# 你的第一首歌曲！ | 简体中文 (1.3.x) | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu.md)
.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#ni-de-di-yi-ge-xuan-l)

你的第一个旋律


------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#xuan-qu-yue-qi)

选取乐器

打开 LMMS后，它向你展示的就是默认的视图布局，里面有歌曲编辑器、样式编辑器和项目笔记等子窗口。首先，点击侧边栏（默认在左边）的预设图标（![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FFq2Z07VQCBAnvwHPgVPh%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df6658b78-8118-4c41-a7af-2ba18480f59f&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d435a848&sv=2)），双击 TripleOscillator（或点击其左侧的三角形符号）展开该文件夹，你会看到各种各样的乐器。按住鼠标点击任意预设可以预览其声音。

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FAnJza0CR6DnM518QjA3r%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dcb4384f8-3e98-4e96-8c84-9df42ecdcc1a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=582c02e&sv=2)

我的预设

接下来，让我们拖拽 TripleOscillator 下一个名叫“Xylophon”的预设到歌曲编辑器中。歌曲编辑器会由此创建一条新的乐器轨道，这个乐器轨道就是你刚刚拖进去的预设乐器。

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FP6pKnbt0bAEP8qJ9FOkD%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D0a74f9f8-4e55-4db7-a806-74e8fb0e679d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3523bc70&sv=2)

拖入一个预设

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FZuKIetljrE7QwViQCAJC%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db81c70ef-5618-4e29-b0a7-898c1829f755&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=11a147c&sv=2)

拖入后的预设

当然了，如果你拖的是一个打击乐器（而非旋律乐器），我们更建议把它拖到样式编辑器中，原因稍后会解释。这里我们是希望在钢琴窗中演奏出一段旋律，所以不会使用样式编辑器，而是使用歌曲编辑器。

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#bian-xie-yi-ge-xuan-l)

编写一个旋律

现在我们要添加音符，以此编成你的第一段旋律。左键单击 Xylophon 轨道中的第一个格子，这样你会在歌曲的第一拍创建出一个块，这个块比其他格子颜色更突出些（![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252Fx0LmuB6lVKx1lUTtbqWC%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D961aa65f-caa1-4f01-a9da-efb51ac0182c&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ba3c3ce4&sv=2)）。双击这个块，可以唤出一个叫“钢琴卷帘窗”（简称钢琴窗）的窗口，我们将在这个窗口中创作旋律！

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252F9zdnpH1lCvmimtzntpfu%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D7a5efc1f-f473-4d57-b9bc-ac4874d72ba6&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ac92bdaa&sv=2)

钢琴卷帘窗

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#hui-zhi-ni-de-di-yi-ge-yin-fu)

绘制你的第一个音符

现在，让我们左键单击 A4 这条线（即 C5 线向下三条线、三个半音那条线，见下图）的第一个黑格子。此时会出现一根绿色的东西，这就是一个一拍长的音符，它的起点就是你单击的黑格子。

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252Fu3lemOBb6HFCV2gDNkq4%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da91a4d9e-d45a-48f3-bdb3-01d34f93b2f7&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=fda4dc24&sv=2)

你的第一个音符

不过这个音符还是太长了！如果我们需要把这个音符的时值缩短到半拍甚至 1/4 拍又该怎么办呢？其实方法很直观，我们只需将鼠标指针移到音符的右边缘，直到指针变成左右双向箭头，然后按住左键拖拽就可以了。我们用这个方法把音符向左拖，直到音符只占两个格子的时值（每个格子占 1/16 拍）。

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FwHDtf2xRvnGC43bueDgS%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D88027167-f6bc-4f11-9914-cd3ba9d9749f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6be78c6c&sv=2)

改变音符的长度

第一个音符创建后，我们希望后面的音符也是同样的时值。 每创建一个音符，系统是需要决定将要创建出来的音符的时值是多少的。而钢琴窗默认采用上次接触的音符的长度。你可以更改这个默认的值，只要在钢琴窗顶部的下拉栏控件（ ![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FMYmbcN4yJFz0kNaS1lN9%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd41a8e0b-73f1-41b5-a794-e0dd3c881fa7&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c1acf001&sv=2) ）操作即可。 不过大多数时候我们其实都只会在默认的“Last note”下工作（这真的很方便）。所以如果有时候你发现它并没有正常工作，那么最好检查一下这个下拉栏。

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#wan-cheng-qi-ta-yin-fu)

完成其他音符

让我们继续绘制旋律，就像下面这样：

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FygMkV7xwnNzHCFv7Kz44%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D25a16d03-3760-4f9b-9519-07834f101300&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3ee72955&sv=2)

“散落”的旋律型

如果你要在第 1 拍放置一个音符，那么应该点击第一拍的 **首个** 黑格子，LMMS 会识别点击位置和节拍线左侧的一小部分区域比前一个节拍更接近第几拍。你可以使用“Q 设置”（量化设置，是个下拉栏，在 ![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252Fwcu07rQckgG7tVl3hi00%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D26c63e2f-882c-4bca-aa86-9842365b0a86&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c5984292&sv=2) 左侧）来调整这个精度。默认情况下，Q 值是与音符长度关联起来的，值为“音符锁定”（Note Lock）。改变这个值，可以调整黑格子（吸附点）的精度。如果你不小心放错了音符，可以直接按住音符的 中间（或左侧）并拖动，以此改变音符到正确的位置。

对了，如果需要删除音符，右键它就好啦。

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#fu-zhi-copy-he-nian-tie-paste)

复制（Copy）和粘贴（Paste）

现在，我们想在下一个小节的开头重复你做好的七个音符。有三种方法可以做到这一点：

*   第一种也是最糟糕的方法，就是直接在后面一个个创建正确的音符——那很麻烦了。
    
*   第二种，也是第一种真正可以考虑的方法是使用复制工具。首先选中你想复制的音符（按住 Ctrl 不要松开，然后拖动你的鼠标画出一个矩形框来选中音符），接着按住 Shift 然后拖拽你选中音符中的一个，这样你就可以拖出已选中音符的副本。此时不要松鼠标，把这个新拖出来的副本拖到你想要的地方。
    

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252Fdzx9Oa9X1mDa48lYnCi3%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D312c8aba-ca07-4abe-b211-fd1c7c213db6&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=84323d1d&sv=2)

音符被选择

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FSIyKg97T5InsqOL7gnfh%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D53a7a259-7646-49b9-b441-9c512bf2c996&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4d5e9982&sv=2)

按住 Shift 然后拖动被选中的音符以复制

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FDCr2i1sIH4KCihjQRYn4%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da269ffda-9d8f-4c3e-8542-6af6caff5f75&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=cff443d8&sv=2)

音符复制好了

*   或者，第三种方法，你可以使用传统的复制粘贴方法。首先按住 Ctrl 选中你要复制的音符，然后按 Ctrl+C 。接着把播放头移动到你想复制到的地方的开头，然后直接 Ctrl+V 。
    

当然了，我们也支持用 Ctrl+A 来选取所有音符。

现在，我们就可以按钢琴窗里的播放按钮（ ![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FrOTun1AZAMUkX7hjbjsD%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D5c8a667e-5eaa-4b0e-b4ea-d8b97c7e3c4a&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4a8e2b5e&sv=2) ）来播放我们写好的旋律了。当播放头运动到有音符存在的最后一小节的末尾，会自动返回到第一小节的开头。

等一下！

到目前为止，当我们演奏旋律时，我们意识到旋律实际上是从错误的节拍开始的。实际上旋律的开始应该是第二个 A4 音符（也就是旋律的第三个音符），那么它之前的两个音符则应该从前一个小节的最后开始。这意味着我们需要将所有音符向后移动！ 按下 Ctrl+A 选择所有音符。然后移动它们，以使第一个音符从正确的节拍开始 (结果见下图)。

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252F5tMPv4egekyGpqq6ngQE%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2aa3a857-c34e-40dd-a8e1-649cb9736766&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b9a0a9b&sv=2)

现在我们需要做更多的排错，尽量处理掉更多的“等一下”。这就好像给你的项目做体检一样，实际编曲中我们经常会做这些，不要担心什么，大胆去做！

第一次保存项目，LMMS会提示你输入项目名称，取名看你喜好，没什么规范。你还可以决定项目是应该压缩（.mmpz）还是保存为未经压缩的 XML 文件（.mmp）。现在就做出你的选择并保存你的项目吧！

保存时，请留意观察屏幕底部的“已保存”通知小窗。

确保你在每次保存都看到这个信息。当你结束编曲要关掉 LMMS 前，如果你执行了保存操作，我建议等待 10 秒左右，等它保存完。看到“已保存”通知小窗并关掉 LMMS后，你也可以查看一下磁盘文件，确保项目已经保存好，大小可靠，时间戳正确。

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#ni-de-di-yi-ge-si-xiao-jie)

你的第一个四小节

如果你对自己有信心，则可以继续添加音符，来创建完整的前四小节（直到主旋律可以重复播放不突兀）。下面是一个示例，如果担心出什么错，你可以按照下面的来写：

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FaglAaPn4KfT8P8SyNVbs%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De1ce223a-7585-442c-9a65-b98784f2ea22&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b20a5abf&sv=2)

“散落”的旋律

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#geng-duo-de-jie-gou)

更多的结构！

写完旋律后，我们就可以关掉钢琴窗，返回歌曲编辑器了。你会看到我们之前创建的块扩展了体积，以适应我们添加的音符。

现在，我们想复制这个块，来填满接下来的四个小节。最简单的办法是按住 Ctrl ，然后把这个块拖拽出一个副本到后面。注意副本一定要拖到“母块”的外面。过程就像下面这样：

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FH3vYUXkd1ayC3ZLo49m5%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D64685596-eec2-4060-b2c4-e32a91cbc533&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=108dddb2&sv=2)

复制块

将该块放置在第 6 小节上。实际上这会晚一小节，因为这个块的前四分之三小节是空的，而上一个块的最后四分之一小节也是空的。幸运的是，块之间可以重叠，重叠的音符会同时播放。

你应该避免让块重叠，因为这会导致上层的块挡住下层块的部分区域，从而让你项目出错的概率的增加。

拖动你刚刚复制的副本块，使其开头在第 5 小节。

现在，你就可以使用歌曲播放器中的播放按钮来演奏这首曲子了。开始播放会经历一小段的静音，然后旋律就会开始被演奏。如果你按照上面的流程一直做下来，你应该可以听到 LMMS 流畅地演奏了两遍你的旋律。

现在歌曲编辑器应该跟下图基本一致：

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252Fepw5vBSUSxkxfyV2MKDH%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1f66a72c-f63a-4e87-b7cf-254edcb00ef7&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4bc54be7&sv=2)

现在的歌曲编辑器是这样的

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#chuang-zuo-ling-yi-zu-xuan-l)

创作另一组旋律

现在我们可以在钢琴窗创建另一组旋律，以此填充歌曲编辑器中剩下的八个小节。

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FNUjyTpnHayHlCRTFmCwR%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd0c0f30d-e7a3-47eb-ba72-1556ed53379b&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=168baa7&sv=2)

对上一组旋律的变体

由于这个作品是块重叠着块，所以你需要先在一个新的小节中创建这个新块（例如第 11 小节），然后再将它移动到与前一个块重叠的位置。

请注意，钢琴窗存在一个小问题会偶尔导致您在打开新块时，会从第 2 小节开始编辑。为了避免后续混乱，请事先检查一下你正在哪个小节中写音符！

现在你的歌曲编辑器应该跟下图基本一致：

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252F1gqIqlUIdA8vjYt9LtZ3%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2bbf8f21-06d7-4a74-a200-386a9d51076a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=30e950cb&sv=2)

到目前歌曲编辑器的样子

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#ni-de-di-yi-ge-jie-pai)

你的第一个节拍


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

你的第一个节拍（鼓组）将利用 LMMS 的内置样本库内的采样完成。 点击侧边栏中的我的采样按钮（ ![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252F2NmEvaOg8LvgbQsIYDld%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D0b11fd61-6b22-4e0c-9886-1ad4905da19d&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bb503c79&sv=2) ），在展开的文件列表内。双击以展开 drums 文件夹。里面是各种鼓的采样，点击他们可以听到对应的声音。

你应该有留意到一个叫“样式编辑器”的子窗口，如果找不到它，可以看向左上角的工具栏。

让我们回到侧边栏中的采样库，请选择一个 **Kick drum**（底鼓）采样（任何一个都可以）。双击采样，便能将这个采样加入样式编辑器内。加好底鼓采样，请再加一个 **Closed HiHat**（闭镲）采样和一个 **Snare**（军鼓）。一切妥当后，你便在样式编辑器中获得了三个乐器轨道。你可以在样式编辑器里，通过点击轨道头右侧的灰格子（ ![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FRQgzZ93iM7gVBBG8oCpG%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D11e55794-a85d-4e82-8877-7186b44e99bf&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ce2f2d1d&sv=2) ）来制作鼓组样式。你也可以点击播放按钮（ ![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252Fyb7r6ngp8d9PPzE7tRML%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D4cc199d5-0cef-495a-bafc-e42e2c679ae6&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=aa32c0e1&sv=2) ）来播放你写好的鼓点，样式编辑器将循环播放它们，直到你停止播放。

下面给出了一些经典的鼓组节奏型，你可以把它们直接用到项目里！

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#jie-zou-xing)

节奏型

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#si-bu-di-gu-four-on-the-floor)

四步底鼓（Four on the Floor）

一个很经典的节奏型，是 Disco（迪斯科）、House（浩室）和 Techno（高科技舞曲）的通用节奏型。

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FnkJtpd1K7C9Hm2mNpvxw%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db453920b-9fba-408e-876f-eff3637247f5&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=777ae702&sv=2)

每拍一个底鼓

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#amen-break)

Amen Break

这个节奏型来自一段传奇的鼓独奏采样，最初由 Gregory Sylvester Coleman 创建。这段采样在 Jump-up DNB（Drum&Bass）和 Jungle 等曲风中被使用过近乎无数次。

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252Ft12GaKxjut0U6zzGFpaR%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db5982cb4-884a-4365-acbc-c74f4ab231c3&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b3ca7a2c&sv=2)

Amen Break

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#jing-dian-sui-pai-breakbeat)

经典碎拍（Breakbeat）

通过让底鼓脱离平常的 4/4 拍框架，我们便得到了一组“碎拍”（Breakbeat）。

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FPwJVwaZRFaV9imHgL2kp%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D826d5be8-b259-4111-ba34-f40dd708fb53&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=5047605f&sv=2)

经典碎拍

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#prodigy-smack-my-b-up)

Prodigy - Smack My B\*\*\*\* up

来自 The Prodigy《Smack My B\*\*\*\* up》的碎拍前奏。

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3710990805-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FZRacsG2wvxpXwri0XH6G%252Fuploads%252FRfjabFNwpuL2kvX5vc2V%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9f59e9af-1d21-4da4-a3d0-b72c5f7de93d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=1d5d4fd0&sv=2)

Prodigy - Smack my B\*\*\*\* up

注意上面两个示例中军鼓音量的降低，也就是某些灰格子的亮度变暗。要更改单个格子的音量，请将鼠标悬停在其上，然后滚动滚轮。对应步进的亮度会随之变化，变暗则音量降低，变亮则音量提高。

想统一听听这些节奏型听起来是什么效果吗？[点此可以下载演示工程](http://docs.google.com/uc?authuser=0&id=0B49dcRGtm8vJVkhEWFJCb3FsTnc&export=download)
 ，里面包含了以上所有节奏型。

要制作出色的打击乐，光有上面的节奏型还不够，我们肯定要走很长的一段路。这里只讲解了在样式编辑器中直接添加简单鼓点的方法。但你还应该阅读“编写低音线和鼓序列”这一部分，其中介绍了更高级的方法。你将学习如何在钢琴卷帘中调整量化（quantizing）以及进行人性化（humanize）处理。

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/ni-de-di-yi-shou-ge-qu#good-song-bad-result)

Good Song! Bad result.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

这些还远远不够！

一个最常见的问题是声音的削波和糊音问题。这些问题会导致曲子听起来就好像用劣质音响放出来的一样。有时候失真确实很容易惹人不高兴，然后第一反应就是去把一些乐器的音量拉低，以图解决这些问题。

所以写完一首曲子，你就要确保自己的曲子不会出现这些问题。这个过程跟你做曲子的过程一样重要。

可以瞄一眼混音台。混音台里会列出所有通道的 dB 量表。 首先，你需要确保在播放你的曲子时，这些通道——哪怕只是 Master 主通道——的声音（在无伤大雅的情况下）没有“见红”，如果声音强度太大，会导致量表“进入红色区域”，失真也就来了。这种声音大部分时候非常糟糕，事实上，甚至还可能损坏扬声器——你的耳朵也是（）

我用“无伤大雅”这个词，是要表达出一个意思，就是你的 dB 量表偶尔“见红”确实没啥关系，但是你要把握见红的“度”。这个“度”需要你自己去慢慢攒经验，慢慢去悟，从而找到真正适合你的那个”度“。

如果你的曲子削波了，你可以用很多不同的方式来处理它。在后续章节。你会了解有关限制器（Limiter）和压缩器（Compressor）等知识，它们都能有效处理这些问题。

当然了，你也可以在网上搜些混音教程、更深入地了解 LMMS，这些都有助于你积攒经验，解决你遇到的问题。

现在，试着调整音量滑块，来去掉”见红“的问题吧。如果真的解决了，再听一遍曲子，你应该能够听到一些区别：乐器的声音应该更清晰，在整体混音中表现也应该更好！

[Previous导出你的歌曲](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/bian-qu/dao-chu-ni-de-ge-qu)
[NextAudioFileProcessor](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/jian-ti-zhong-wen/yue-qi-cha-jian/audiofileprocessor)

# FAQ | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/faq.md)
.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/faq#id-1)

About


------------------------------------------------------------------------

**What is LMMS anyway?**

LMMS is part of a family of softwares known as digital audio workstations, or DAWs. It is a sound generation system, synthesizer, beat/bassline editor and MIDI control system that can power an entire home music studio. With LMMS, sounds and tones can be generated, played, and artfully arranged to create entire tracks with ease. The tools needed to arrange sounds and rhythms to create tracks, layer tracks to create songs, and save songs to an output format are all there, ready for you to use. With a MIDI keyboard, you can also play your music live while composing or performing.

**Is LMMS comparable to any commercially available software?**

Users may find LMMS has much in common, at least in principle, with Apple's Garage Band, FL Studio (formerly Fruity Loops), Logic Studio, Beat Machine Pro and similar powerful audio tools. In terms of capability, LMMS easily matches the lower to middle-tier professional software available. This makes LMMS a great introduction to audio production. LMMS is also constantly developing.

**Is LMMS offered in my language?**

Possibly. See [currently supported languages.](https://github.com/LMMS/lmms/wiki/Creating-a-localization#currently-supported-languages)

**Is it free?**

Yes. LMMS is free and open source. This means that as long as there are people coding for LMMS on their own free time, LMMS will become better and better!

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/faq#id-2)

Usage


------------------------------------------------------------------------

**How do I create an instrument track in the Beat/Bassline Editor?**

Drag it in from the sidebar as you would with the Song Editor! You can open _My Samples or My Presets_ (buttons on the far left side of the window) and double click on the preset to get it there too.

**How do I change the note an instrument plays in the Beat/Bassline Editor?**

Click on the instrument button on the track. There's a green bar just above the keyboard at the bottom, an orange square indicates the key that is played. Click on the blue bar to change the note.

**How do I enable and adjust looping points?**

Click on the _enable/disable loop points_ button in the song editor or piano roll. It is disabled by default. Two blue arrows will appear in the upper tracking area of the window. You can adjust these loop points by right-clicking and dragging them or pressing middle and right button.

**I made some piano chords in the Song-Editor but now I want to loop them. How to get them to the Beat+Bassline Editor?**

Simply hold Ctrl while dragging the instrument handle into the Beat+Bassline Editor. Only the first pattern will follow.

**How do I import a drums beat I'd made in Hydrogen?**

As of LMMS 0.4.14, you can import your Hydrogen file directly into LMMS. If you exported it as a MIDI, it can be easily imported with Project > Import. Remember to specify a SoundFont file! If on the other hand, you exported it to WAV, you'll have to use the AudioFileProcessor plugin and import it from there. You can import your MIDI the same way too.

**How do I import a FLAC, WAV or OGG file?**

Through the AudioFileProcessor plugin.

**Can I sell my song that I made in LMMS?**

Yes. As long as you have just used samples, instruments and effects from LMMS, or know that the external material has correct licenses, you can sell your song created in LMMS.

**How do I edit the locations in the sidebar when you open a file in LMMS?**

Highlight the folder that you want to be on the sidebar, select it, and drag it to the sidebar.

**What do I do when it is pops and clicks at the beginning or end of a note?**

Turn up the attack or the release knob.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/faq#id-3)

Settings


---------------------------------------------------------------------------

**I want to use another soundcard than the default one with LMMS. How to achieve this?**

It's recommended to set your preferred audio-interface and it's parameters using the setup-dialog. If this doesn't work for some reason, you can also set the environment-variable "AUDIODEV" to whatever your audio-device is (e.g. _/dev/dsp_ for OSS or _surround40:1,0_ for ALSA).

**I want to import MIDI files but it tells me I need to specify a default soundfont?**

First download a soundfont for free from the internet. Then in LMMS go to Edit > Settings > Paths (folder icon). Under the "default soundfont file"-section, you can browse for the downloaded file.

[PreviousWelcome to LMMS](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual)
[NextInstallation](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/installation)

# Installation | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/installation.md)
.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/installation#min-system-requirements)

Minimum System Requirements


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   **OS:** Windows 7, MacOS X Lion, Linux
    
*   **CPU:** 1.5 GHz x86, x86\_64, or ARM-based CPU with 2 cores
    
*   **RAM:** 1 GB
    
*   **Available Storage Space:** 100 MB
    

LMMS may run on systems that do not meet these requirements, but its performance or stability is not guaranteed.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/installation#recommended-system-requirements)

Recommended System Requirements


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   **OS:** Windows 10, MacOS X High Sierra, Linux
    
*   **CPU:** 2 GHz x86, x86\_64, or ARM-based CPU with 4 cores
    
*   **RAM:** 4 GB
    
*   **Available Storage Space:** 512 MB
    

More CPU cores, higher CPU clock speed, and additional RAM will increase LMMS's capacity to handle large projects and more plugins.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/installation#installing)

Installing LMMS


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

LMMS is available for [Windows](https://lmms.io/download/#windows)
, [macOS](https://lmms.io/download/#mac)
, and [Linux](https://lmms.io/download/#linux)
.

While packages are available for many Linux distros, the AppImage provided at [lmms.io/download](https://lmms.io/download/)
 is the preferred installation method.

As of LMMS 1.3.0-alpha, 32-bit (x86) builds of the software are no longer released, and its support has been deprecated.

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/installation#stable-beta-and-alpha-builds)

Stable, Beta, and Alpha builds

Stable, Beta, and Alpha builds may be downloaded at the links above, or through the [GitHub releases page](https://github.com/LMMS/lmms/releases)
.

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/installation#nightly-builds)

Nightly builds

Nightly builds offer bleeding-edge features and bug fixes, at the cost of stability or performance. These builds are mainly intended for testing, but they are able to be downloaded and installed like any other builds nonetheless.

Nightly builds may be downloaded at the links above, or directly through [GitHub Actions artifacts](https://github.com/LMMS/lmms/actions/workflows/build.yml)
, choose a build output that isn't failing, then head to the "Artifacts" section in the Actions page. Note that a GitHub account is required for downloading them.

For Windows builds, LMMS offers two build artifacts, MinGW and MSVC.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/installation#compiling)

Building LMMS from Source Code


---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Visit the [LMMS development wiki on GitHub](https://github.com/LMMS/lmms/wiki/Compiling)
 for instructions on how to compile LMMS for Linux.

# Reporting a Bug | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/reporting-a-bug.md)
.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/reporting-a-bug#bug-tracker)

Bug Tracker


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

For reporting any kind of bugs, use the [GitHub issue tracker](https://github.com/LMMS/lmms/issues)
. Please take a look at other [submitted bugs](https://github.com/LMMS/lmms/issues)
 before opening a new item as someone else might have reported the same bug.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/reporting-a-bug#gdb)

Reporting Bugs with `gdb`


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you are using LMMS and encounter a segmentation fault (crash), please include a full backtrace:

Linux

Copy

    gdb /usr/bin/lmms
    r

Then attempt to replicate the crash. Once LMMS crashes:

Copy

    thread apply all backtrace

The output of this command is interesting for us! Please either report it to the [issue tracker](https://github.com/LMMS/lmms/issues)
 or email it to the [mailing list](http://lists.sourceforge.net/lists/listinfo/lmms-devel)
.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/reporting-a-bug#reporting-bugs-without-gdb)

How to Report Bugs without GDB


-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Linux

Copy

    $ ulimit -l unlimited
    $ lmms

Now when LMMS crashes, there will be a file called "core" in your working directory. The file may be large enough to be worth zipping.

[PreviousTroubleshooting](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting)

# Interface Overview | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/general-interface.md)
.

There are 11 main parts to the LMMS interface. These are described in detail in the following chapter. This section gives a brief description of each part of the interface. It also lists the sections of this guide in which each part of the interface is explained in greater detail.

For information on the Settings window, refer to [this page](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/setup)
.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/general-interface#menus)

Menus


---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The [**Main Menu Bar**](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/menu-bar)
 serves as a general menu for LMMS.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2F1pi2ocUtRh1t5OtuCFGk%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ce3251a6&sv=2)

Main Menu Bar

The [**Toolbar**](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/toolbar)
 contains options for managing project files, showing/hiding interface windows, and altering master controls for projects.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FXltmnsDtjSlIh9mJD8fy%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=fe5bdc1&sv=2)

Tool Bar

The [**Sidebar**](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/sidebar)
 is used to introduce resources to a project.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2F6twKc7oRalV6y5UFbkOW%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=13d1aeb6&sv=2)

Sidebar (Instrument Plugins tab)

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/general-interface#windows)

Windows


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Most of the work you do creating songs in LMMS will be done in the program's windowed GUI. Each type of window has a specific purpose.

The [**Instrument Window**](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/instrument-window)
 contains settings for an instrument.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FnonerSs0ZR6SPon8tGdZ%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=cc3204a5&sv=2)

Instrument Window (AudioFileProcessor)

The [**Song Editor**](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/song-editor)
 contains all the different sound-generating elements and automation clips in a project file.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FFDh4jF3B3dQ1j04AQQbw%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a0207f7e&sv=2)

Song Editor

The [**Beat+Bassline Editor**](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/3.8)
 is where repetitive loops, such as drum or basslines, can be created and contained. It is explained in.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FQFAFqaG8SU0TZHJZIGVP%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f59db21&sv=2)

Beat+Baseline Editor

The [**Piano Roll**](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/piano-roll)
 allows you to create and edit note sequences.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FzoSu0i0Y1Q40srK4uuMp%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=2e567934&sv=2)

Piano Roll

The **Automation Editor** allows you to create a profile that any connected parameter can follow. It is explained in [section 3.9](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/automation)
.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FmzpTZQr6S4r2vPpJuxKm%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=8f47910c&sv=2)

Automation Editor

The **FX-Mixer** is where FX channel routing and manipulation takes place. It is explained in [section 3.10](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/fx-mixer)
.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2Fz2LNKRwuOfIlP4FFlKJU%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=430d1473&sv=2)

FX-Mixer

The [**Project Notes**](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/project-notes)
 window allows you to create notes for yourself in a project file It is a simple text editor.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FUNHie5hxAnaijpYvkEvQ%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3becc9b&sv=2)

Project Notes

The [**Controller Rack**](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/controller-rack)
 is where controllers are added and configured. These controllers allow you to make alterations to connected parameters, much like an Automation Clip would.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FXqxIG8Ns5Q8EITHVIMjD%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bf821ea2&sv=2)

# Safety | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/safety.md)
.

As mentioned in General settings, the **Backup** feature is ON by default. It is highly recommended to keep it that way! You may also like to invoke the **Autosave** feature. Whereas a _.BAK_ file represents the penultimate version of your work, Autosave will _create a copy_ of the _current_ file state.

Autosave will only be invoked when LMMS is _idle_, so it will not by default happen in replay or during export unless specified otherwise in the settings! You control the interval between each Autosave. Because an autosave results in a tiny drop in performance, you find the settings for using Autosave in Settings > Performance. A choice of 5 - 15 mins is recommended. The default is 5 mins. If LMMS crashes it is very important that you pay attention to the screen next time you open LMMS! You will get 2 options from Autosave:

1.  **Ignore:** anything Autosave has kept will be deleted!
    
2.  **Recover:** LMMS will load the Autosave version of the last loaded project as a recover project! You need to use File > Save as and rename the recovery file to a name of your own choice! Do that immediately!
    

Remember to save often! Save your project every time you've created something that sounds great. It's also a good strategy to save different versions of your song, allowing you to revert to a previous version of the project in the event that you've tweaked the production into something you don't like.

LMMS provides a special version control option for this in the File menu: _Save as new version_. Let's say your project was named as "MyProject", the new version will be named "MyProject-01", and the old file will not be altered. You can also use the _Save as_ menu's plus (+) and minus (-) buttons to increment the number suffix.

When you save a project for the first time, you will be prompted for a folder and project name. You also have to choose if your project should be compressed (mmpz) or uncompressed (mmp). Subsequent saves will overwrite this file, and LMMS will show you a small message at the bottom of the screen.

In the event that LMMS crashes and is unable to recover or detect its last autosave, or if the project gets corrupted somehow, or even when you accidentally break or change your current project irreversibly, you may be able to recover your work by using the aforementioned _.BAK_ file. Remove the _.BAK_ suffix from the file, and the project may then be opened normally. Note that the revision stored in these backups are LMMS's last saved backup, and may be much older than the most recent manual save or autosave.

Wait for the message, before you continue your work!

[PreviousController Rack](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/controller-rack)
[NextJumping Right In](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/jumping-right-in)

# Troubleshooting | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting.md)
.

Make sure you are running the latest version of LMMS before troubleshooting issues. The latest version can be found at [https://lmms.io/download](https://lmms.io/download)
.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#general-troubleshooting)

General Troubleshooting


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#my-settings-wont-save)

My settings won't save

Some users report that they can't save their settings. This is a recurring Windows issue, with reason not known in depth. To solve this problem, follow these steps:

*   Close LMMS. Make sure nothing from LMMS is running in the background. You can look for this in your "Task Manager". (If you don't know how to use "Task Manager", restarting your PC is a good alternative.)
    
*   In versions older than Windows 8, "Documents and Settings" is the folder you are looking for. In Windows 10, look in your "C:\\Users\\<Username>\\. There is a file called `**.lmmsrc.xml**`.
    
*   Use the search tool to find this file. **.lmmsrc.xml**You _can't rename_ this file, but you _can move_ it.
    
*   Move it to one of the folders you have there, e.g. **plugins**. Now restart LMMS.
    
*   You should be now asked to enter new settings, and these settings should be persistent.
    
*   A terminal command version of this process exists:
    
    *   Run CMD as administrator
        
    *   Paste this command:
        
    

`move %USERPROFILE%|.lmmsrc.xml %USERPROFILE%|.lmmsrc.xml.bak`

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#lmms-is-taking-forever-to-load-a-project)

LMMS is taking forever to load a project

Try adding exceptions in your antivirus for files the project depends on, such as:

*   LMMS.exe
    
*   RemoteVstPlugin.exe
    
*   Plugins and their related data
    

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#audio-troubleshooting)

Audio Troubleshooting


---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#my-audio-is-crackling)

My Audio is Crackling

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#buffer-size)

Buffer Size

Your [buffer size](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/settings#buffer-size)
 may be too small, resulting in buffer underruns. To fix this, increase your buffer size. Note that this will increase latency.

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#audio-interface)

Audio Interface

Certain [audio interface configurations](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#audio-interface)
 don't play nice with LMMS on some systems. If you are on macOS or Windows, make sure you have SDL selected. On Linux, if PulseAudio is producing these unwanted artifacts, try ALSA instead.

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#track-volume)

Track Volume

Your audio within LMMS may be too loud, resulting in digital clipping. This can be fixed by reducing the volume of the instruments in LMMS (either with the "VOL" knobs or through the mixer).

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#volume-envelopes)

Volume Envelopes

If an instrument has attack or release values small enough, the rapid change in volume can sound like clicks or pops at the beginning or end of each note. Increasing the attack and release values for the culprit instrument's volume envelope can fix this kind of clicking. Ensure that the envelope amount (AMT) is set to full.

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#i-cant-hear-anything)

I Can't Hear Anything

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#dummy)

Dummy

Certain [audio interface configurations](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#audio-interface)
 (specifically Dummy) just don't work. If you are on macOS or Windows, make sure you have SDL selected. Try to avoid PortAudio if possible.

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#jack-transport)

JACK-transport

If you have JACK selected as your audio interface and you are experiencing no audio output, JACK-transport might not be running. Start an application like `qjackctl` and click on "play" or its equivalent. LMMS should automatically should start JACK-transport on startup, but this fails on some systems.

You may also need to connect LMMS to your sound card, using the patch bay in `qjackctl`.

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#the-audio-lags-or-is-delayed)

The Audio Lags or is Delayed

#### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#buffer-size-1)

Buffer Size

[Buffer size](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/settings#buffer-size)
 is directly related to latency. If you can, try decreasing your buffer size to minimize latency.

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#my-effects-especially-echo-or-reverb-are-not-working-smoothly)

My effects, especially echo or reverb, are not working smoothly.

**Effects running without input**

Go to Edit > Settings > General settings (Gear icon). There is a toggleable option "Keep effects running even without input". Make sure this option is _enabled_. If it is disabled, your echo and reverb will stop being active when they no longer detect an input audio, and this can cause them to cut off.

If you do not wish to enable this option for any reason (usage of certain very specific noisy plugins being a primary use case), turn up the "Decay" knob of the effect (located next to the "W/D" amount knob) displayed in the effect chain.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#video-troubleshooting)

Video Troubleshooting


---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#lmms-has-very-small-windows)

LMMS has very small windows

**LINUX**

Try using a shell script:

Set `chmod +x <script name>` and then make a symlink in a desired location.

You have to add all your connected monitors to QT\_SCREEN\_SCALE\_FACTORS in order to make it work with all connected monitors.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#development-troubleshooting)

Development Troubleshooting


---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#lmms-segfaults-and-there-are-no-error-messages-on-the-console)

LMMS Segfaults and There are No Error Messages on the Console

Use the GNU Debugger to run LMMS again, and try to replicate the crash:

If a segfault happens, LMMS will freeze and you'll get some information on the terminal. Entering `bt` or `bt full` will show a backtrace you can report.

Make sure you have debugging symbols in LMMS. That usually means compiling with the `-g` flag and not stripping the symbols afterwards.

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/troubleshooting#i-compiled-lmms-with-vst-support-but-it-doesnt-work-at-all)

I Compiled LMMS With VST-Support, but it Doesn't Work at All

Make sure you didn't pass `-fomit-frame-pointer` in a FLAG-parameter to configure. This is known to lead to an unusable LVSL (LMMS VST Support Layer).

[PreviousSettings](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/settings)
[NextReporting a Bug](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/getting-started/reporting-a-bug)

# Project Notes | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2Fac7FuFomOw4d994OBMM3%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=74f859e2&sv=2)

Project notes window

In LMMS there is a notepad in which you can write useful info. It can be for yourself regarding the project or for others to see if you share your project.

It includes these tools:

*   Font type
    
*   Font size
    
*   Bold
    
*   Italic
    
*   Underline
    
*   Text align
    
*   Color
    
*   Undo
    
*   Redo
    
*   Copy
    
*   Cut
    
*   Paste

# Creating a Track | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/creating-a-track.md)
.

The standard way to create a track is to add percussion instruments to the Beat+Bassline Editor and instruments for melody tracks into the Song Editor. Then create different percussion loops using the **Add bassline** button in the Beat+Bassline Editor for each rhythmic element you want - a main bass line, a hihat line, special rhythms and breaks, etc. These are listed in the Song Editor as they're created. To make them play, click on the start bar in the track's timeline to create a new **block element** (segment). You can also drag in Beat+Bassline blocks to make the pattern go across multiple bars. The same technique can be used for repetitive bass lines, if you create them in the Beat+Bassline Editor.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FV9vxRKp9LirxFJxjAqtG%2Fkeyboard-piano.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d41bb32d&sv=2)

Keyboard as piano

For melody tracks, click in the Song Editor on the bar where you want a new melody segment and then double-click that block element to open the Piano Roll Editor. Place your notes in the piano roll, by clicking on the notes wanted start "position" or **tick**, to create a new note, you can then drag the notes right-hand edge to be as long as you want. You can also drag notes up and down to change their pitch.

Alternatively, you can press the record button in the Piano Roll Editor and play notes in real time, either on the computer keyboard or a MIDI keyboard.

We recommend that you as next read and do the exercises in the section Your First Song with LMMS.

[PreviousInstruments: Samples & Presets](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/samples-and-presets)
[NextUsing MIDI](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/midi)

# Beat+Bassline Editor | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/3.8.md)
.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FzUuI72FtBlWfoGe4NtLY%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f029c4b5&sv=2)

Beat+Bassline Editor

The Beat+Bassline Editor is the main window for sequencing rhythms and repetitive musical lines in LMMS.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/3.8#toolbar)

Toolbar


-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FPLOz9RSgKd9ZLhh9n656%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=973c0130&sv=2)

Beat+Bassline Editor toolbar

**Play** and **Stop** buttons allow you to play and stop the currently selected Beat/Bassline (BB) track. When **Play** is pressed, it will be replaced with a **Pause** symbol allowing you to pause the playback rather than stop it entirely. **Stop** returns the play-head to the first step in the BB track; **Pause** allows you to continue from the last played step. **Add steps** and **Remove steps** allow you to add or remove the number of steps in a bar.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FF7ioIzX8xzhmWo2iVUUi%2Fimage.png&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=650ab565&sv=2)The drop-down menu allows you to select the BB track to edit. It displays the current **Beat/Bassline (BB)** **track** being edited.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FzJdQM9GHFmESikb4VARd%2Fimage.png&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ecfb9fb6&sv=2)The **Add Beat/Bassline** button creates a new Beat/Bassline track in the Song Editor, numbered sequentially from "Beat/Bassline 0". Right-click on the new BB track's name in the Song Editor and rename it to something useful.

All BB tracks are linked in such a way that they share the same instruments.

If you delete or toggle-mute an instrument in one BB track, the effect will be applied in all BB tracks within this project. So, if you have multiple BB tracks in your song, remember that all the BB tracks will contain the same instruments. This means that you'll have to enforce silence on those instruments that you want silenced in a particular BB track. For example, say you have a first BB track with a kick drum, snare, and HiHat, and a second BB track with just a clap and a shaker. All 5 instruments will be in both BB tracks. You'll want the clap and shaker instruments in the first track to be silent, so in the first track, for both the clap and shaker, you can either

*   Make sure all the steps are turned off (see Context menu clear below)
    
*   Context menu mute the track (toggle mute won't work because the instrument will be toggle muted in all other BB tracks)
    

Also, you'll have to do the same thing to the kick, snare, and HiHat in the second BB track to make sure those 3 are silent.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FLqSQKLl9ja2LMV3WWt2K%2Fimage.png&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a080450d&sv=2)The **Add automation track** button adds a new automation track to the current BB track.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/3.8#instrument-track)

Instrument Track


-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2F7AH8jqS1wMKozPwlODFI%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=acb033f0&sv=2)

Instrument track

Each instrument added to the Beat+Bassline Editor will look like this. The track settings you recognize from the Song Editor are on the left side and the Step Editor is on the right (the gray boxes).

### 

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/3.8#step-editor)

Step Editor

By default an instrument track has 16 steps (16 gray boxes), 4 steps to each beat in a 4-beat bar.

Each step icon is both an on/off toggle and a volume knob. A left-click on a step toggles it on or off. To change its volume, hover your mouse over step and raise or lower its volume by rolling your mouse scroll wheel up or down. The step is bright green at full volume, gray at zero volume, and an intermediate brightness of green at intermediate volumes. A left-click will still turn the step on or off, and the step remembers its last volume setting. Editing the volume of a step in the Piano Roll (see below) is also reflected in the color brightness of a step.

When a step is turned on, the sound from the instrument will play at that step in the bar.

The 4 steps in each beat group are shaded alternately light and dark gray to make orientation easier.

Right-clicking on a step gives you the context menu for that track.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FBpfkBzvXxt1HBPp5zCs0%2FBB%2520editor%2520context.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=2a2497fa&sv=2)

BB track context menu

*   **Open in piano-roll** allows you to edit the notes of a segment in the Piano Roll Editor rather than just as steps. This allows you to place notes with more than 1/16th precision, and to vary the pitch, length, and volume of the notes (steps).
    
*   **Copy** and **Paste** allow you to copy a set of steps from one track to another by copying the original track's steps, right-clicking on the new track and choosing Paste
    
*   **Mute/Unmute** allows you to context menu mute/unmute the instrument in this BB track only. Unlike the toggle mute/unmute switch (the light), this method does affect the same instrument in other BB tracks in the same project.
    
*   **Clear all notes** allows you to quickly erase all the steps in this track
    
*   **Reset name** resets the segment name to the track name, and the name is again hidden
    
*   **Change name** allows you to change the name of this track
    
*   **Add steps** and **Remove steps** allow you to add or remove the number of steps in a bar
    

[PreviousSong Editor](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/song-editor)
[NextPiano Roll](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/piano-roll)

# Instruments: Samples & Presets | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/samples-and-presets.md)
.

Now we take a look at those components that create sounds: samples and instrument presets. To insert one of the many factory pre-defined instrument presets into the Beat+Bassline Editor or Song Editor, click the 4. button in the sidebar. It works just like a browser. You can now choose a preset and drag it into the song editor. Everything that you can do in the sidebar browser with presets, you can also do with samples (just click the 3. button in the sidebar). Furthermore, you can click and hold your mouse on the name of a preset or sample. LMMS will then let you listen to that particular instrument, as a kind of sound test. You can then drag the instrument into the editor.

If you add VST instruments to your preset collection, you should _never_ use this preview feature on these VST presets!

You can also double-click an instrument in the browser. This will place the objects directly into the Beat+Bassline Editor, or you can right-click on the instrument in the browser list tree, and LMMS will open a context menu. Here you have the option to send this instrument into an empty slot in the Song Editor or to replace a current active instrument with this new instrument. The active instrument is the one that is highlighted in the Song Editor, and has its GUI opened, in other words has been mouse-clicked.

Always use the send to active instrument option when ever you need to load a VST instrument preset (more on this: How to use VST instrument presets).

When you insert an instrument, the instrument's GUI editor window will open. You can also see this GUI by clicking on the instruments grey name button in the Song or Beat+Bassline Editor. When you click the name button, it will change to lightgray. This instrument is now the active instrument. The name button acts as a toggle for the GUI editor window. Instrument GUI's have five tabs: PLUGIN, ENV/LFO, FUNC, FX and MIDI. The PLUGIN tab changes with the type of instrument. All other tabs are standard for every instrument, however the ENV/LFO tab is only available on the kind of instruments that can use this feature. VeSTige, LB302 and ZynAddSubFX plugins do not present this tab. An in-depth explanation of how to use the features on the various tabs can be found in the Working with instruments section.

If you want to remove an instrument completely from your song, click on its gray toolkit icon and choose "Remove this track".

Instruments are divided into factory provided and user specified. Subfolders in your file system can be used to organize the instruments or samples hierarchichally.

Later you may like to create your own instruments from scratch. The sidebar's 1.button opens the instruments plugins. These are empty default preset templates that you can use for your own instruments. Drag them into your project and play around with all the settings on the GUI. Some hints and tips on how to create 3-OSC instruments can be found in Making TripleOscillator patches. You can of course also make changes to the factory-defined instruments and save those as your own. Overwriting existing factory-defined instrument is not recommended.

[PreviousJumping Right In](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/jumping-right-in)
[NextCreating a Track](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/creating-a-track)

MIDI Settings


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FVy4s8CaqcX1qk8iulLRG%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=706a77a0&sv=2)

MIDI settings

ALSA-Sequencer is the recommended setting for Linux. WinMM MIDI is the default setting for Windows. Modify the default settings only if necessary.

Plugins MIDI

You can read on how to connect a MIDI device to an instrument in the MIDI tab section.

# Controller Rack | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/controller-rack.md)
.

Controllers are used to vary the position of a knob or other control over time using another signal such as an LFO waveform. They differ from automation because automation uses a shape drawn manually and usually the shape is not a repeated pattern. In contrast, LFO controllers use waveforms to yield a regular pattern.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FJLLYI7Xk8IwhpSpdK7T7%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c631dc2&sv=2)

Controller rack

The Controller Rack displays all the controllers created within this project.

There are 2 types of controllers:

*   LFO controller
    
*   Peak controller
    

The right-click context menu allows you only 1 options: "Remove this controller". You can change the names of the controllers by double-clicking on the current controller name.

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/controller-rack#lfo-controller)

LFO Controller


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

LFO stands for **Low Frequency Oscillator**.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FzZ07Dvm7WZmiD1jgqtuG%2Fimage.png&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=dbe811d9&sv=2)This button is the Controller Rack show/hide toggle button (F11). To add an LFO controller entry to the Controller Rack, click the Add button at the bottom of the window.

To edit the settings for a particular LFO controller entry, click the Controls button on the right side of the entry. The LFO controller settings are:

*   8 waveform option buttons
    
*   4 knobs
    
    *   BASE: the center (or zero line) around which the wave oscillates
        
    *   SPD: speed is the rate (frequency) of wave oscillation
        
    *   AMP: amount is the size (amplitude) of the wave
        
    *   PHS: phase offset is where in the waveform the wave starts its first oscillation
        
    
*   3 speed-multiplier option buttons
    

[](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/controller-rack#peak-controller)

Peak controller


---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A Peak controller allows you to use the volume peaks from an audio source to automate the values of a control. A Peak controller is similar to Gate, but with greater flexibility. Peak controllers commonly use the output signal of a percussion track to control the volume of a sustained sound on a different track. This results in the sustained sound being chopped up in time with the percussion. In LMMS, when you add a Peak Controller it is visible in the Controller Rack, but it's actually an **effect** applied to an FX-channel. If you click on the "Controls" button you'll get a window telling you to use the FX-channel's controls (magenta arrow). Therefore, to get to the Peak Controller settings, click the "Controls" button on the Peak controller entry in the Effects chain box (yellow arrow). Beware an FX-channel can have more than one Peak controller entry.

[PreviousProject Notes](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/project-notes)
[NextSafety](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/safety)

Was this helpful?

*   [LFO Controller](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/controller-rack#lfo-controller)
    
*   [Peak controller](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/navigating-lmms/controller-rack#peak-controller)

# Jumping Right In | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/jumping-right-in.md)
.

A project is created by adding instruments (sidebar tab 1), samples (sidebar tab 3) and/or presets (sidebar tab 4) to either the Song-Editor or the Beat+Bassline-Editor. Each added element is in LMMS regarded as a **Track**.

In this way you can have instrument tracks based on presets, Beat+Bassline tracks and automation tracks.

The Song-Editor is useful for non-repeating sound events, such as melody lines, while the Beat+Bassline-Editor is useful for repeating sequences, like percussion.

Events created in the Beat+Bassline-Editor are "continuous", i.e. they can't be expanded (but can be copied, of course).

The actual song is always built in the Song-Editor. This is where all the **elements** (Song-Editor blocks), including events in the Beat+Bassline Editor, come together on the timeline forming the project.

When starting a new project in LMMS there are 4 items loaded in the Song Editor by default. The first one, named "Default Preset", is an instance of the TripleOscillator (3-OSC) generator; it's a good example of an instrument container. This TripleOscillator instance's default settings generate sounds using waveforms, default it is simple sine waveform, but that you can change. Let's take a look at this instrument, because it has behavior that is the same for all other instrument containers. When you move your mouse over the instrument containers, they will highlight. This indicates that the instrument can be clicked as if they were buttons! Left-click the 3-OSC. This opens the instrument GUI. The 3-OSC instrument is now chosen, and it is at the same time the focused instrument. Click again, and the GUI will close. All instruments behave like this.

When you make changes on the GUI you create a preset. If it sounds less than horrible, you have an instrument-preset :) If you right-click you will have a rename option. _Always name your presets!_ To the left of the instruments there are 2 clickable lights (![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FghEVNzjGnjdORvIRvPbI%2Fimage.png&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=7ee93a46&sv=2)). When the green light is ON, the track is activated. In its OFF state the track is muted. The other light is red. When it is ON, the track will mute all other tracks and play solo.

Next, to the left of these, we have the action-button (![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FF81C7DVr8sopMlvaS6zc%2Fimage.png&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=188ba701&sv=2)). Here you can delete or clone the track and all of its events. To the right of the Instruments, before the knobs, we have a vertical bar. When clicked, it will play the root note of the instrument. We also have a dial for volume and one for balance:

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FcOQaduEfvbLOJX9ikn1g%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=8d193b14&sv=2)

Volume and panning

In most great programs, there are always more than one way to do a specific task! This makes various workflows possible. This is also the case with LMMS. The volume dial we just looked at, is a good example. You can adjust volume with several different controls. In the main component we have the main sound settings, called the "master volume".

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FERF9KAIyraKDEQYxnZtW%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bfe023fc&sv=2)

Master volume

You would really never change this setting for creative reasons. But if the phone rings... The same goes for master pitch. Creative changes in volume is best done in other controllers. The volume dial on your instrument, that is suited for controlling the volume on a saved instrument preset. The volume sliders in FX-Mixer are suited to set volume in a project. Remember that everything you like to save for general usage, that must be saved with the preset! This goes for the aforementioned volume, but also for effects added on the FX-Tab. Amazing FX chains that you build in the FX-Mixer will not be simple to use in a new project! If, however, it is made directly in an instrument preset, then it is natively usable in any project, as a saved instrument preset! Planning and making good decisions can spare you a lot of fruitless redoing! I will also recommend you to use the project notes. Write down your ideas, because it is next to given, that you will not remember where in the project you had that "neat little phrase".

On the right half of the Song Editor, we have the timeline. Create a track by adding events to this timeline. The timeline is divided in bar-sized blocks. Click in one next to 3-OSC. You get a visually different "box". If you double-click this box, you open the Piano-Roll Editor. This is where you compose. You can also right-click in the box and choose the context menu option _Open in Piano-Roll_. We will return to that later.

Below the 3-OSC you have the Sample Track. It is great for vocals or other larger WAV or OGG files that you want to replay unaltered, but can also be used as an advanced clip looper. You can add a new sample track with the 6. button on the Song Editor toolbar (![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FoL0Oc3tRpU4xhMtIUf5L%2Fimage.png&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d872cd2b&sv=2)).

Next you have the default Beat+Bassline instance. Just for illustration, click on one of the boxes in the timeline next to the Beat+Bassline track. You get a blue block. Click and drag its right end and it will expand. This is what I meant by Beat+Bassline events are "continuous". When you did that with the 3-OSC, the block would not expand. You can add a new Beat+Bassline with the 5. button on the Song Editor toolbar (![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2Fj63p5LRV3dPdE4WniH1C%2Fimage.png&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d8e28cec&sv=2)).

Last is an instance of the **Automation track**. This can give you advanced variations in real-time, and it is with automation that you can create nice subtle changes in the sound over several bars, known in trance and house music, or rhythmic repeated growls and effects for, say dubstep. How to do all this and how to use automation tracks is explained in Working with automation. You can add a new automation track with the 7. button on the Song Editor toolbar (![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FARi3tuc9KvzilPpfUM9o%2Fimage.png&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3d63a494&sv=2)).

[PreviousSafety](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/safety)
[NextInstruments: Samples & Presets](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/production/samples-and-presets)

Was this helpful?

Was this helpful?

# GIG Player | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/instruments/gig-player.md)
.

The GIG Player or GigaSampler Player plugin in LMMS uses files called gigasampler files (.gig).

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FcPRIouF0ShDF3dMESM8p%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=fdd41d80&sv=2)

GIG Player

Initially a format for similar purposes to the [soundfont](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/instruments/sf2-player)
, used by the GigaSampler and Gigastudio project, the .gig file is the basic instrument unit, and would load as Instruments. It stores the collections of samples (and any data associated with their playback) within the Instrument file to allow their playback and usage. A GIG file is capable of holding up to 128 instruments.

Additionally, the software and by extension some GIG Instruments also support a feature called keyswitching. The GIG Player of LMMS does not support keyswitching presently. Additionally, where GIG files would treat individual instruments like separate MIDI banks, LMMS loads and displays GIG files' instruments as patches due to the better applicability of the term.

This was a format that could access the primary instruments from hard disk rather than RAM, allowing it to handle larger instruments- hence the prefix "Giga".

The GIG file format is now obsolete, and the Instrument files are not commonly available or widely used. It was developed a proprietary format, and the Giga technology and the rights to the trademarks currently belong to Garritan Company, founded by Gary Garritan, but activity on the technology is unknown.

Garritan now uses sfz files, which allow a lot of the functionality that GIG instruments can. SoundFont files and sfz files are recommended instead of these files.

LMMS implements GIG file support using libgig from LinuxSampler.

[PreviousFreeBoy](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/instruments/freeboy)
[NextKicker](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/instruments/kicker)

# PatMan | User manual

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/instruments/patman.md)
.

Patman is a GUS-patch (.PAT) player.

![](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2FsOyi2widgEtSqcjOGVN2%2Fblobs%2FLYMrIX0E3eqeZ5rGCYWr%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f27f7147&sv=2)

Patman

GUS patches are patches associated with Gravis Ultrasound soundcards. Their concept is very similar to SoundFont and they can be considered SoundFont predecessors.

Patman is a simple patch loader. It also allows looping of the patch. There aren't many patches online, but you can find a pretty good collection [here](http://freepats.zenvoid.org/)
.

[PreviousOrganic](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/instruments/organic)
[NextSF2 Player](https://docs.lmms.io/user-manual/instruments/sf2-player)
